It walks the published site now                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [356](issues/356-she-came-back-to-her-page-and-two-panes-spun-forever.md)                                         | major    | She came back to her page and two panes spun forever                                                     | fixed                             | History and Preview both read their document out of the studio session — right, and documented — but drew "named but not open" as `<PaneWaiting>`: a spinning mascot with `role="status"` under an instruction, forever. It is what a refresh normally produces, since both open `beside` their document and both are restored before anything opens it. Now the console's own "nothing there" shape, carrying the button that opens the document the address already names; `controller.open` re-focuses the restored pane rather than making a second one, so one click resolves it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [357](issues/357-half-the-times-in-her-version-list-were-broken-across-two-lines.md)                              | minor    | Half the times in her version list were broken across two lines                                          | fixed                             | A fixed `w-14` (56px) against a clock: `2:59 AM` measures 55.8px and `2:44 AM` 56.9px, so one pixel decided whether a row was 20px or 40px tall, and every save between 10am and 1pm (66.1px) wrapped every time. `tabular-nums` + `min-w-18` + `whitespace-nowrap` in a shared `when.ts`, which also absorbs the duplicated day and clock formatters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [358](issues/358-her-busiest-page-said-nobody-had-ever-opened-it.md)                                              | major    | Her busiest page said nobody had ever opened it                                                          | fixed                             | Her home page read 0 people / 0 opens with no address, at the bottom among pages nobody visits; its 20 visits were in the "addresses no page owns" line, which named cart and sign-in as examples while both are rows in the table above it. `slug != null` treats the legacy NULL home slug as no address, when the storefront's own resolver treats NULL, `''` and `/` as the same one. The report now picks home the way the resolver does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| [359](issues/359-the-report-told-her-every-page-she-built-had-sold-nothing.md)                                    | major    | The report told her every page she built had sold nothing                                                | fixed                             | All 22 rows read `Bought 0 (0%)` while she had 14 orders in the window: 0 of them carry an attributed landing path, so the answer was "we cannot tell" and the screen wrote zero. `AttributionCoverage { placed, traced }` now separates the two and the money columns render dashes with the reason. The same-day attribution window behind it is FOLLOW_UPS #9                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [360](issues/360-ten-of-her-pages-were-listed-at-an-address-with-two-slashes.md)                                  | minor    | Ten of her pages were listed at an address with two slashes                                              | fixed                             | `` `/${slug}` `` against a slug that already carries its slash: `//book`, `//cart`, `//account/login`, and all ten of them pages the platform made for her. Fixed at the write AND healed on read, so no Rescan is needed. `normalizePath` is deliberately not the place: it never strips a leading slash, because `//host/path` is a protocol-relative URL. The twelve check names came with it, rewritten out of JSON-LD, canonical, sitemap.xml and Indexability                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| [361](issues/361-the-set-up-screen-crashed-every-time-it-was-opened.md)                                           | blocker  | The set-up screen crashed every time it was opened                                                       | fixed                             | `useStoryModel must be used within a StoryModelProvider`, on both onboarding surfaces, every open, for everyone. The provider has exactly one reference in the app: its own definition. The gate that used to mount it is gone, and the reopenable surfaces that replaced it took the flows without it. Typecheck, lint and every check were green throughout                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| [362](issues/362-the-set-up-screen-spoke-another-products-language.md)                                            | major    | The set-up screen spoke another product's language                                                       | fixed                             | Rows called CMS, CRM and AI · MCP, a rail starting "Modules", TTFB and headless SDKs in the detail, **fourteen other companies named outright**, and a plan-updates-as-you-flip sentence on a product with one flat plan. Rewritten in the rail's own names and her language; `DEFAULT_ON` deleted; and `check:piggles-nav` given a third seam for plain catalogs so it cannot report green over the first screen again                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [363](issues/363-a-ready-made-design-deleted-every-page-on-the-site-it-was-added-to.md)                           | critical | A ready-made design deleted every page on the site it was added to                                       | fixed                             | Two sentences promised nothing would be replaced; `installSite` syncs with `allowReplace: true` and deletes every stored page. Proved on a site I made: two designs added, the first one's 9 pages all gone while its install still reads `installed`. The picker defaulted to her live primary site, and it cannot be undone. `/v1/properties` now carries `pageCount`; `installImpact()` writes one truthful sentence that drives the field text, a `danger` alert, the button and the confirm                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [364](issues/364-the-set-up-screen-offered-to-rebuild-a-business-that-was-already-running.md)                     | critical | The set-up screen offered to rebuild a business that was already running                                 | fixed                             | Both setup flows open on a pristine salon and neither reads a fact about the business it is pointed at. One press turns off seven apps she uses, lays a salon template over her site and renames the business. There is no first-run gate here, so the menu is the only door and everybody who opens one already has a business. `isBusinessRunning` + `<SetupGate>` now send a running business to the screens that own each piece                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| [365](issues/365-adding-the-same-design-to-a-second-site-broke-halfway-and-left-the-site-behind.md)               | major    | Adding the same design to a second site broke halfway and left the site behind                           | fixed                             | Three natural-key lookups scoped per site against unique indexes that are per business, so any second install died on the first author — while the pane says “you can add it to more than one”. The site was already made and the picker never moved onto it, so the failure read as “that address is taken”. Reconciled on the enforced key, picker settles the moment the site exists, and the existing second-site test now carries a byline so it can see this shape                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [366](issues/366-a-whole-app-existed-and-there-was-no-door-to-it.md)                                              | major    | A whole app existed and there was no door to it                                                          | fixed                             | Campaigns had surfaces, a hue and a nav slot, and no app claimed the module — so All apps, which says “Everything Piggles does”, did not list it and the rail had no door. Four more copies of the same list were stale the same way, one of which meant funnels could not be switched off for a single site. Campaigns is an app, the per-site list is derived, and `check:piggles-apps` now asserts both halves                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [367](issues/367-she-said-dont-keep-me-signed-in-and-the-console-kept-her-signed-in-for-a-month.md)               | major    | She said “don’t keep me signed in” and the console kept her signed in for a month                        | fixed                             | The console mints its own cookie and never saw the checkbox, so it used the attribute `getCookies` hands back — `session.expiresIn`, thirty days, for everybody. The choice now travels with the handoff token, read from Better Auth’s own `dont_remember` cookie, and the marker is set on the console’s domain so a later refresh cannot restore it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| [368](issues/368-the-sign-in-screen-said-it-had-emailed-her-a-link-and-had-not.md)                                | major    | The sign-in screen said it had emailed her a link, and had not                                           | fixed                             | With no `EVENT_BROKER` set the logging stub discards the event and `publishEvent` resolves, so both passwordless routes reported a delivery that never happened. `Publisher.discards` makes that answerable, `publishAuthEmail` refuses the four templates somebody is waiting on, and the account app’s `.env.example` finally names the broker every other publishing service documents                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| [369](issues/369-on-her-phone-nothing-said-the-trial-was-ending-and-then-the-site-would-go-dark.md)               | major    | On her phone, nothing said the trial was ending, and then the site goes dark                             | fixed                             | The rail card warns well and is mounted inside a rail that collapses, and that phones do not have, so two whole populations were told nothing before the site went offline. The account menu the code pointed at carries a link and no state. A band in the slot both shells already mount, words shared so the two cannot drift                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [370](issues/370-her-last-chance-shelf-was-empty-and-always-would-have-been.md)                                   | major    | Her “Last chance” shelf was empty, and always would have been                                            | fixed                             | Four faults in a row. Setting a reorder point never resynced the flag it feeds, inventory events never reprojected rule-driven collections, nothing was published downstream, and the indexer skipped every variant-keyed event because it read `productId` off payloads that carry only `variantId` — nine of its nine listed types. So a shelf whose rule is “running low” could not contain the size that had run out, and the search index never moved on a stock or SKU change either                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| [371](issues/371-nobody-could-buy-two-of-anything.md)                                                             | critical | Nobody could buy two of anything                                                                         | fixed                             | The quantity box in every buy box was frozen at 1 — typing, the arrow keys and the spinner all did nothing, so a customer could buy one of any item and no more. HTML's `value` (where a control starts) walked into React's `value` (what it is pinned to), and a pinned field with no `onChange` never moves. The older renderer already handled it; the newer one did not inherit it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [372](issues/372-she-named-her-form-and-her-inbox-still-called-it-something-else.md)                              | minor    | She named her form, and her inbox still called it something else                                         | fixed                             | One form, listed as two: the messages that arrived before she named it wore the page address instead. The name was snapshotted onto each row at submit, so renaming a form only ever applied going forward. Resolved from the form now, once, for both the column and the picker                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

The length caution measured the short string too                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [374](issues/374-every-tab-inside-a-product-forgets-itself-on-reload.md)                                          | minor    | Every tab inside a product forgets itself on reload                                                      | open (scope)                      | Seven tabs, no address: reload or send the link and it opens on Overview. Params are the pane's identity, so a `tab` param would fork the pane rather than re-address it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

The checklist counted publishing, not reading                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| [376](issues/376-every-shop-on-the-platform-started-with-three-posts-that-had-no-date.md)                         | major    | Every shop on the platform started with three posts that had no date                                     | fixed                             | 67 undated published entries across 19 tenants — every tenant has exactly three. Postgres sorts NULLs FIRST on `ORDER BY published_at DESC`, so they outrank everything a shop ever writes, and the card's date line renders empty. Three write paths bypassed the CMS service's own correct handling; a `CHECK` constraint now makes it impossible                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| [377](issues/377-adding-a-design-published-somebody-elses-articles-to-her-site.md)                                | critical | Adding a design published somebody else's articles to her site                                           | fixed                             | The install screen promises three times that nothing goes live until you publish, and its example articles arrived **published** and publicly served. On a site with a live journal that puts made-up press releases on a real business's site instantly. Pages and products already followed the installer's own stated draft contract; all 243 content entries across the 191 designs declared `published`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [378](issues/378-searching-her-own-site-could-not-find-a-single-thing-she-wrote.md)                               | major    | Searching her own site could not find a single thing she wrote                                           | fixed                             | "No results for 'returns'" on a site whose footer links a Return Policy. The `cms_page` projector read the deprecated `Page` model (0 rows platform-wide) and articles indexed under `cms_entry`, which public search excluded — so the strip that was built to show them could never be non-empty. Index went 30 → 238 documents                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

| [387](issues/387-nine-strangers-were-the-only-names-she-could-put-on-her-own-writing.md)                          | major    | Nine strangers were the only names she could put on her own writing                                      | fixed                             | Authors on her shop listed nine writers from two magazine designs installed on OTHER sites, and the same list fed the byline picker — so those nine were the only names she could sign her own work with. `authors.property_id` and its index shipped and no read in the 217-line route ever filtered on it. Scoping it also needed a This site / All my sites control, because a byline's address is unique per business and a copy is refused                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [388](issues/388-she-put-her-name-on-her-writing-and-the-page-still-said-nobody-wrote-it.md)                      | major    | She put her name on her writing and the page still said nobody wrote it                                  | fixed                             | She picked her byline, saved, and her name appeared zero times on her live post. The API serves it, the site types it, `projectByline` puts it in scope, and the masthead binds date, title, excerpt and stops. `cms.ts`'s own header had written the remedy down and deferred it, and the deferral outlived six other pieces of the feature                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

Now `PATCH /v1/redirects/:id`, a clickable row, one dialog for both jobs, and a duplicate that hands her into the rule she collided with.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| [397](issues/397-the-same-failure-was-announced-twice-and-the-red-one-never-went-away.md)                         | major    | The same failure was announced twice, and the red one never went away                                    | fixed                             | The failed-write net cannot see a surface that RENDERS its error, so 59 call sites got a second toast saying the same sentence with no remedy — and `timeout: 0` meant it outlived the failure, sitting beside the success that replaced it. `shownInPlace` + withdrawal, both in `@wizeworks/query`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| [398](issues/398-on-a-phone-the-button-squeezed-the-message-to-seven-pixels.md)                                   | major    | On a phone, the button squeezed the message to seven pixels                                              | fixed                             | `.alert` never wraps and `.alert-actions` never shrinks, so a 194px button in a 253px alert left the message 7px and drew over it. One brand-blind rule in silica-corrections; the flex-basis half is what keeps the desktop layout identical.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| [399](issues/399-one-line-she-already-had-threw-away-the-whole-import.md)                                         | critical | One line she already had threw away the whole import                                                     | fixed                             | A refused row aborts the Postgres transaction the good rows are in, so a JavaScript catch cannot save them: 500, nothing added, and a per-row skip report that could never be reached. SAVEPOINT per row plus the duplicate pre-check the single create already had; the route also now publishes `redirect.added`, which it never did.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

Silica splits "selected" across two elements and the fill — a separately measured sliding pill — was `hidden`, leaving ink chosen for a filled pill on nothing. Height pinned, both consoles; the resting list already filled it, so nothing was lost                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

Now `note` → `session.pieceNote()` → the row, live-first, with two tests                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Now a button with the app's modifier contract; its `neutral` badge went colorless                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

That call is for naming a RECORD, not for re-typing a static title. Deleted in both consoles                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |

Plus 346px of table in a 323px box at 360px, clipping the Languages header: [390] again, on a list that never adopted `IDENTITY_CELL`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

Records now rank on name, then subtitle, then the server's order; nothing dropped                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [423](issues/423-the-other-console-had-no-tests-and-four-fixed-defects-were-still-in-it.md)                       | major    | The other console had no tests, and four fixed defects were still living in it                           | fixed                             | `sparx/apps/workbench` had no vitest config, no `test` script and no vitest. Giving it the seat and porting five test files found four fixes that landed in piggles and never crossed: the setup guard ([364]), unmeasured file sizes ([380]), untraced sales, and a failed write blaming the connection ([386]). 44 tests pass there now; the consoles stay copies, never a shared package                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| [424](issues/424-the-other-console-had-the-parts-but-had-never-been-given-them.md)                                | major    | The other console had the parts and had never been given them                                            | fixed                             | The parity list, measured by depth rather than by name. Four items: PaneLoadError built in sparx and used twice against 200 here (a deleted record read "the server could not be reached" over a Try again that can never succeed); every VALIDATION_ERROR swallowed, so a service explaining a business rule arrived as "Check what you entered"; 307 toolbars still passing children, so nothing could fold; and no screen at all behind the uncosted-stock warning. Two more on the way: ⇧-click silently opened a tab from every piece of chrome, and five ctx.open calls pointed at nothing. Windows mode was a REVERSED DECISION, not a gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [425](issues/425-a-green-badge-told-her-she-was-taking-payments-when-nothing-was-charged.md)                      | major    | A green badge told her she was taking payments, when nothing was being charged                           | fixed                             | Manual payments is switched on and charges nothing. The row badge said "Active — taking payments" in green, the pane header said "Taking payments", and the detail alert said it a third time — six lines under the pane own sentence "nothing is charged online". The fact was already served (checkout: none) and simply not read. gatewayState now separates switched-on from charging, and while in the file the two grey "neutral" pills that meant different things were split (RULE #4)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
