The shelf name has a job (telling that entry from the platform gateway) but is not the company whose dashboard she opens. GatewayDescriptor carries processor now                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| [427](issues/427-her-delivery-groups-did-nothing-and-she-could-not-put-anything-in-one.md)                        | blocker  | Her delivery groups did nothing, and she could not put anything in one                                   | fixed                             | rateShipment never looked at which product group a rate belonged to, so every group price reached every basket — a $25 coat surcharge offered on a basket of scarves, and a cheap group rate would have been offered on every order in the shop. And nothing anywhere could put a product IN a group: no control on the product, no API route, on a screen that promises "you add products to the group from each product later". The rule is now one tested module (20 tests); the product carries shippingProfileId; defaultProfileId is one function both screens read                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| [428](issues/428-her-shop-charged-no-sales-tax-and-the-screen-said-it-was-collecting.md)                          | blocker  | Her shop charged no sales tax at all, and the screen said it was collecting                              | fixed                             | taxService.calculate had NO callers anywhere — not checkout, not the cart, not B2B — so taxTotalCents stayed 0 on every order the platform has taken, under three green Collecting badges. Measured three ways. Tax is now priced beside shipping in submitShipping; taxRegionCode translates "California" to US-CA and says nothing rather than guessing; certificates are honoured and record WHICH one applied. 18 new tests                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [429](issues/429-her-denver-studio-was-told-it-had-staff-in-california-texas-and-new-york.md)                     | major    | Her Denver studio was told it had staff in California, Texas and New York                                | fixed                             | The clothing starter installed three state tax zones as nexusType physical, isActive true, so the console asserted a presence she never claimed and set her collecting on it. Harmless only while nothing charged tax; fixing [428] made it real money the same afternoon. Every pack now installs switched off and says so; an off place states no reason. Act 94 then fixed the RULE rather than the instance: only a signed-in person may switch collection on, enforced in the schema default, the service, a database CHECK and the calculator, with every unclaimed place switched off by migration                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| [430](issues/430-she-could-sell-a-gift-card-and-nobody-could-ever-spend-it.md)                                    | blocker  | She could issue a gift card and nobody could ever spend it                                               | fixed                             | The whole redemption path was unwired: applyGiftCardToCart and redeemGiftCard both had zero callers, there was no API route, no box at checkout, and appliedGiftCardCodes was hardcoded empty — while the pane promised “share it with the recipient so they can spend it at checkout” under a green Ready to spend. One box now takes a discount OR a card; applying reserves and placing the order debits; the card books as an OrderPayment so Still owed is right. The code is finally shown on its own pane                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [431](issues/431-a-design-update-could-rename-her-business-and-nothing-would-say-who-did.md)                      | major    | A design update could rename her business, and nothing would say who did                                 | fixed                             | A caterer’s name sat in her site’s brand override. Not a tenancy leak: it came from a blueprint, and the installer already refuses to carry businessName/tagline (issue 210) — but the UPDATER, its twin, read them, merged them and wrote them back, and an automatic merge always takes theirs. So a refreshed design could rename her business silently. Found the second half while asking who wrote it: /v1/properties audited NOTHING, so a site could be renamed, re-branded, promoted or deleted with no record. Both fixed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| [432](issues/432-a-customer-bought-something-and-heard-nothing-back.md)                                           | blocker  | A customer bought something and heard nothing back                                                       | fixed                             | The order-confirmation email is provisioned, published and live on every shop, and NOTHING has ever sent it: zero automations trigger on order.placed platform-wide, no seed names the template, and checkout sends no mail. The four order emails that do exist were written as "the counterparts to order-confirmation that were missing", on the assumption it was handled. Worst on a pay-later shop, where the confirmation IS the handover and the checkout promises "we’ll be in touch about paying for it". One seeded automation; existing shops picked up by the daily reconcile, no migration                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| [433](issues/433-she-marks-an-order-sent-and-the-customer-is-never-told.md)                                       | blocker  | She marks an order sent, and the customer is never told                                                  | fixed                             | Sweeping the whole email set after [432] found shipping-confirmation had no sender either: order.fulfilled fires and 55 automations listen, every one of them the review request that waits three days then asks how they liked a parcel nobody announced. Two more underneath, both the same twin-path drop: entityRefsFromFields carried no fulfillmentId, so a split shipment mailed the first box the second box's tracking number, and no bookingId, so an owner's own booking automation would send a confirmation with date, time and service blank                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| [434](issues/434-the-order-number-broke-in-half-and-the-money-fell-off-the-edge.md)                               | major    | The order number broke in half and the money fell off the edge                                           | fixed                             | In a docked pane the Order column got 83px while a badge column got 118, so O-000016 rendered as "O-" over "000016" and "Due Thu, Sep 10" wrapped to four lines with the last clipped. Stopping the wrap exposed what it hid: six columns need 719px in a 676px pane, and the Total fell off the right edge. Placed waits for @3xl now; ten rows visible where five fit. At 360px a `truncate` with no cap (the failure IDENTITY_CELL's own note describes) was still growing the column by 26px                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| [435](issues/435-the-email-told-her-customer-the-parcel-went-by-usps.md)                                          | major    | The email told her customer the parcel went by "usps"                                                    | fixed                             | Found by sending a real order, not by reading code. The shipping confirmation bound {{shipping.carrier}} straight to the stored lowercase column, so the ONE place this fact left the business was the only one not translating it. The translation existed three times and had drifted: consoles said "Sent by the supplier" where her own website said "Drop-ship", the website had no entry for `other` so its toUpperCase fallback showed a shopper "OTHER", and a parcel with no carrier printed the literal word "Carrier". One carrierLabel() in commerce-schemas; three local maps deleted                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| [436](issues/436-the-parcel-went-and-the-tracking-number-had-nowhere-to-go.md)                                    | major    | The parcel went, and the tracking number had nowhere to go                                               | fixed                             | Found by checking whether a toast told the truth. "The customer can follow it from here" showed whether or not a tracking number was given, and with none the customer's email has no tracking row and no track button at all. Fixing the wording promised she could add one later, which was also false: PATCH /v1/orders/:id/fulfillments/:fId has always existed and NOTHING in either console called it, so a shipment was frozen the moment it was made. Same gap the POST half was already fixed for. Built the editor in both consoles; the audit log recorded status only, so an edit that set a tracking number wrote a diff saying nothing changed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| [437](issues/437-a-customer-who-collected-in-person-would-be-told-their-order-had-shipped.md)                     | major    | A customer who collected in person would be told their order had shipped                                 | fixed                             | Mine, from [433], caught by carrying on with the job. A collection is recorded as a fulfillment carried by `pickup` and publishes order.fulfilled exactly like a despatch (deliberately, so the feed and the review request see the sale complete), and my new automation's only condition was `customer.email is_set`. Ravi has no email on file, so nothing went out: luck, not a guard. Now `fulfillment.carrier neq pickup`, which first meant making the field resolvable — the resolver merged only fulfillment.id and now reads the row. Guard checked BOTH ways: removing it turns the test red                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| [438](issues/438-she-shipped-the-goods-and-had-no-way-to-ask-for-the-money.md)                                    | blocker  | She shipped the goods and had no way to ask for the money                                                | fixed, proven end to end          | $1,756.50 owed on eight orders already sent, on a shop whose checkout promises "we'll be in touch about paying for it". Confirmed by DRIVING the form: the New invoice screen has no order field anywhere, so billing $234.60 meant retyping every line by hand and typing a tax rate on a shop that already has tax places set up — and paying that invoice settled nothing, because BillingDocument carried no order column (its one order link, convertedOrder, points the other way: quote to order). Built the join, a createInvoiceForOrder that copies the sale without re-pricing it, the deposit carry-across, and THE SETTLEMENT HOOK so paying the invoice moves the order. Finishing it found the capability could not work: createInvoiceForOrder skipped applyStageEntryEffects, so the invoice had NO NUMBER and sendInvoice refuses an unnumbered document. It also copied the order address shape into billTo, which reads three flat strings, so the Bill to form, the list column, the PDF and the emailed greeting were all blank. Proven as Devi: O-000013 invoiced, emailed, paid, and the ORDER flipped to paid                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| [439](issues/439-the-send-button-said-there-was-no-address-and-sent-it-anyway.md)                                 | major    | The Send button said there was no address, and would have sent it anyway                                 | fixed                             | The confirm read billTo.email alone, blank on most invoices, so it announced "there is no email address on this invoice yet" about one whose customer has one, then offered a Send it button that works — the server falls back to the customer and the screen did not know. One rule in two places, and the copy facing the person is the half that drifted. Server now returns the resolved billedToEmail and both consoles ask it. And the sparx console could not send AT ALL: its two outbound actions both hand the job back to the operator mail client. Same shape as [436] — an API verb with one caller                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [440](issues/440-four-automations-chase-an-invoice-nothing-ever-sends.md)                                         | major    | Four automations chase an invoice that nothing ever sends                                                | fixed                             | Asked the DATABASE, the way act 98 did. Nine invoice automations live on her tenant and NOT ONE fires on crm.billing_document.created; seven invoice email templates exist and none is "here is your invoice". So the platform sends a friendly reminder three days before a bill the customer has never seen is due, then overdue, then a second notice, then a final notice. Not just a seed: service-repair has TWO payable stages and there is no dedupe at the send layer, so the naive rule sends twice. Fixed by making the ladder require invoice.sentAt, and by NOT building the obvious auto-send: an invoice is an EDITED document, so emailing it on create sends one she has not finished. The guard has a cost paid for in the same act — a bill she never sends is now never chased either, so every unpaid row carries a Not sent badge and the list gained a Sent filter. Guard run red: removing it turns the new test red and leaves its twin green                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| [441](issues/441-the-letterhead-is-frozen-on-every-invoice-and-printed-on-none.md)                                | major    | The letterhead was never frozen, and never printed                                                       | fixed                             | billing_documents.issued_by is filled on every finalized invoice with a long comment about renaming a site rewriting the letterhead on invoices already in customers hands. Nothing reads it — not the snapshot payload, not the PDF, not the email, all of which go to the LIVE site name, so the exact thing it prevents still happens. And b2b-ar-service builds its document directly and never sets it at all. I filed it as "written and never read" and the DATABASE corrected me: 0 of 90 documents carried an issuer, 52 finalized. It was never WRITTEN either — the freeze sat in a stageType===final block and the DEFAULT workflow has no final stage. Same mistake, same function, as the due date that was already moved out of that block for that exact reason: one field moved and its neighbour left behind. Fixed in four parts (freeze when PAYABLE, read it when printing, read it when emailing, freeze it on the B2B ledger). Proved by moving her business address and reprinting: still the address it was issued under                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| [442](issues/442-the-delivery-charge-vanishes-from-the-bill-and-the-total-does-not-add-up.md)                     | major    | The delivery charge vanishes, and the bill does not add up                                               | fixed                             | The editor showed Total $58.00 beside Amount due $27.00 with $40.00 paid, and warned "not saved yet" about a document nobody had touched. totals.ts is the browser mirror of the server totals and its own header names the rule it mirrors -- lines, subtotal, discount, tax, SHIPPING, SURCHARGE, total -- while the function under that comment stopped at tax. No Delivery row existed anywhere in the invoicing surface. The emailed summary was worse: no delivery, no surcharge and no DISCOUNT either, so two of the five invoices this shop had ever sent were already in a customer inbox asking for a number their own rows could not reach (INV-000001 $276 asking $234.60; INV-000003 $634 asking $659). The printed PDF had both rows from the start, which is why every check passed -- the one artifact that got it right is the one nobody reads on screen. Fixed in the mirror, the Summary card, the type + normalizer, the emailed rows (now invoiceSummaryRows, pure and tested on the invariant a customer applies: the rows must add up to what is asked for) and the template binding scope. Red both ways: dropping shipping reddens four of five and leaves the fifth -- a document with neither charge -- green, which is why it shipped                                                                                                                                                             |
| [443](issues/443-a-shops-invoice-is-signed-by-the-company-that-makes-the-software.md)                             | major    | A shop invoice is signed by the company that makes the software                                          | fixed                             | Brandon read act 105 rendered invoice and asked whether a tenant bill should be signed off by WizeWorks. It should not, and ACT 105 HAD ALREADY LOOKED AT THAT LINE AND CLEARED IT -- on a test comment reading "the operator, correct for both", where both means both PRODUCTS. Every case in that file renders password-reset, whose reader PAYS WizeWorks. I read the comment instead of the cases. The platform had already decided this and built it in the OTHER renderer: silica/frame.ts signs a shop email with the shop name, the shop address, then "Sent with <product>" -- that is [122], and [122] says the sweep before it fixed the React templates and missed the frame. Now the mirror image. Four templates reach somebody who has never heard of us; three also carried our WORDMARK, because header={false} was a separate lever and one template had ever pulled it. Worst was the signature request: platform chassis, our wordmark, our company, naming NO business, asking a stranger to sign -- the shape of a phishing attempt. Fixed with one REQUIRED audience prop deciding masthead and sign-off together; it immediately caught a ninth call site no grep would have found. Two tests were holding the defect in place, one of which had been slicing the whole document since React Email started inserting comment markers                                                                   |
| [444](issues/444-she-added-a-color-and-five-shirts-went-on-sale-with-no-count-behind-them.md)                     | major    | She added a color and five shirts went on sale with no count behind them                                 | fixed                             | Adding a colorway and pressing "Give them all the same price" put five versions on her website that nobody had counted. A version becomes stock-managed by being COUNTED, not by existing, so they sold WITHOUT LIMIT while their own setting read "stop selling it when you run out". That rule is right and deliberate -- writing a zero row on creation is how "nobody counted this" became "there are none", and how a bakery watched her shop call all ten of her products sold out with bread on the counter -- so the storefront was correct and the console was silent. Stock -> Stock said "Showing 1-15 of 15" for a shirt the products list calls twenty; searching the code said "Nothing matches that". Same shape as always: the pane has a message for a product where NOTHING is counted and the list asks the catalog when a search returns NOTHING, and twelve counted with five not falls between them. Fixed in both: the product pane's summary names the mixed case, and the stock list carries a band above the table (a band, not a filter chip -- a chip is something you have to know to press). Needed a question nobody could ask, since the list starts FROM inventory_levels: GET /v1/inventory/uncounted. 60 versions across 15 businesses were in this state and invisible everywhere. Closed the loop as Devi: Count them -> Record a count x5 -> 78 to sell became 108 and the band went away |
| [445](issues/445-the-count-form-opens-on-a-building-she-does-not-use.md)                                          | moderate | The count form opens on a building she does not use                                                      | fixed                             | Where you counted it opened on Fulfillment Center every time; Juniper Row has 67 count rows in Main Warehouse and ZERO at the Fulfillment Center. It was locations[0] -- the first place by NAME, because F comes before M. Not a guess about her business, a fact about the alphabet. Counting five versions of one shirt meant correcting it five times, and the form did not remember the correction either. The obvious answer was WRONG: commerce_site_settings.defaultWarehouseId exists and all 35 rows on the platform carry a value, but nothing in the console edits it and bootstrapDefaults writes it once by picking the oldest warehouse -- 34 of 35 never updated by anybody. A machine-written guess must not be given authority over a real shelf, so the form reads EVIDENCE: this version's own place, then the place this person last counted at, then where the rest of the product is, then the list. Two above three deliberately, so somebody counting a delivery at the Fulfillment Center is not sent back to Main Warehouse on the next version -- a place chosen ON PURPOSE has to survive. Proved both directions on screen; four separate breaks of the ladder redden four different single tests                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| [446](issues/446-the-uncounted-band-told-her-to-count-33-things-that-cannot-run-out.md)                           | major    | The uncounted band told her to count 33 things that cannot run out                                       | fixed                             | MINE, one day old, and the plainest 'fix leaves its neighbour behind' yet: the audit SQL written into [444] carries AND v.inventory_policy = 'deny' and the query that SHIPPED does not. I measured with a filter I never implemented and reported the measured number as though the shipped code produced it. Her Stock screen read 33 versions you sell have never been counted and named a membership, a field guide and an AI report -- all set to keep selling when out, all unlimited on purpose, nothing to count. The right number was ZERO. Platform-wide it was 1,664 rows against 55: thirty times noise, and a band that names things which do not need doing teaches her to skip the one that says five. The row payload already carried inventoryPolicy and 444's own text says why -- 'the setting that is NOT being honoured' -- carried, documented, never read. One rule now, countingMatters, asked by both consoles' two renderers AND the server: stop-selling-when-out cannot fire while nothing was counted so that is the news; keep-selling-when-out is unlimited as asked; a version never posted has no shelf. Two conditions, two dedicated tests, each the other's green twin                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| [447](issues/447-escape-threw-away-the-color-she-typed.md)                                                        | moderate | Escape threw away the color she typed                                                                    | fixed                             | Typing a hex into the swatch and pressing Escape lost it silently. Enter kept it, clicking away kept it, only Escape discarded -- silica's HEX box holds a draft and commits on blur, and Escape closes the popover without ever blurring. Escape is not a cancel there and CANNOT be: every slider in the same panel commits live, so closing already keeps them, which made the HEX box the one control in its own panel behaving differently, in the direction that loses work. Silent in the worst case too: setting a first color at least leaves the No color picked badge up, while CHANGING one shows the old color, which looks exactly like a chip showing the right one. Now one shared SwatchPicker for both consoles that settles the field on the way out, running the picker's OWN commit rather than re-implementing parsing. Belongs upstream -- ColorPicker should commit its draft on unmount -- and that is written at the top of the file so it can be deleted when silica lands it                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
